# SCOPE: the whole BN6 (Falzar ROM) battle engine, and how "done" is measured

The user (2026-09-14): "The purpose of this project is to replicate the bn6 battle engine ... all the chips, viruses,
Navis, program advances, MegaMan transformations, UI, and so on." This file is the completion ladder. Every item is
done only when it has (a) a canon recording (a scenario in tools/states.py), (b) its routines ported from the
disassembly with citations, (c) state parity on the trace (tools/trace.py: no divergence on the scenario), and
(d) pixel parity (0 differing pixels on the scenario's frames). The counts below are placeholders until T10
regenerates the per-item tables from the ROM's own tables (tools/inventory.py).

Decisions (the user, 2026-09-14 14:00): audio IN scope; netbattle IN scope; Gregar-exclusive content in scope in
principle, but no Gregar disassembly exists (dism-exe/bn6f covers Falzar only, sha1 0676ecd4...), so M11 starts
with a measured ROM diff against bn6f's symbols once the user provides the Gregar ROM (never tracked).

## Milestones

| # | milestone | measured by | status 2026-09-14 |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | Inventory from the ROM's tables (chips, PAs, viruses, Navis, forms, panels, statuses, formations, backdrops, NaviCust battle effects) | the per-item tables below regenerated by tools/inventory.py with counts | T10 open |
| M2 | Engine core on a full battle at zero divergence: sequencer incl. custom-screen states, chip selection rules (codes, Regular, Tag, PA recognition, folder draw/reshuffle), RNG parity in battle, end of battle with rank and rewards, every entry type | battle_full trace: first divergence none; pixels 0 on all its frames | interpreters landed (T4-T8), Mettaur as canon's routine (T6), sequencer table (T7/T7b); T7c open; battle_full diverges on 174/540 sequencer frames |
| M3 | Field and damage system: every panel type and effect, ownership/area steal, obstacles, elements and weakness, panel bonuses, guard/shield/barrier/aura, counter hit and Full Synchro, statuses, invisibility, super armor, flinch/knockback/drag, pierce, hit stop, popups | one recorded scenario per rule, trace + pixels | not started (the Mettaur/chip rows cover a few incidentally) |
| M4 | Chips as data: chip object routines ported in coverage order, the custom-screen chip UI, then every standard chip, mega, giga, secret; Program Advances | per chip: scenario, port, trace, pixels | 14 chips pixel-verified AS DATA -- the sword/blade family-0x13 rows (Sword..BambSwrd, Muramasa, T17), the Barrier family-0x15 subfamily-0x04 rows (Barrier, Barr100, Barr200, T48), AND AirShot (id 4, family-0x21, T52) dispatch through the record's AttackFamily/AttackSubFamily and the ROM's own tables -- plus 29 pixel-verified through our own code; StepSwrd (81) rides the same record dispatch without a pixel row of its own (tools/inventory.py AS_DATA_FAMILIES={0x13,0x15,0x21}) |
| M5 | Viruses: every family and rank | per entry: scenario, port, trace, pixels | Mettaur done; Gunner in progress (T9c) |
| M6 | Navis: every boss in the ROM, the Cybeasts, Bass | per Navi: scenario(s) covering its full pattern, port, trace, pixels | not started |
| M7 | MegaMan's forms and emotion system: Crosses, Beast Out/Over, Cross Beast, charge shots, emotion window states and counter, NaviCust battle effects | per form/effect: scenario, port, trace, pixels | base form only |
| M8 | Presentation completeness: every arena backdrop and palette, all banners and popups, HP displays, results variants (win/lose/escape/time-out) | per item: scenario, pixels | one arena; win results only |
| M9 | Audio: the sound driver's battle side (music, SFX triggers from every routine that plays one), measured by dumping both sides' audio on a scenario and comparing sample-exact | per scenario: audio parity + the triggers' trace | not started (mgba can dump audio; a comparison tool is the first ticket) |
| M10 | Netbattle: the link session (two instances, the handshake, chip trading rules, the netbattle-specific UI and timers) | two-instance recording vs two-instance replay, trace + pixels on both sides | not started (needs link emulation in the capture tool) |
| M11 | Gregar-exclusive content (Navis, chips, Crosses, backdrops) | first a measured diff of the Gregar ROM against bn6f's symbol map (how much code differs, how much is data); then the same per-item standard with the Gregar ROM as a second canon | blocked on a Gregar ROM from the user; no disassembly exists |

Cross-cutting invariants, checked by the daily review: no allowlist entries, no fitted constants, every negative
fixture failing, the verifier cross-family from the worker, coverage closed per scenario (every executed routine
ported or listed out of scope with a reason).

### viruses (M5) (FOUND via T12: byte_80182C4 identity rows + off_8109150 Struct2 (tools/rom_enemy_tables.py))

Note: elem_hp caveat: the Struct2 word is `elem_hp u16 @0x00`; its HIGH nibble is the ELEMENT and the HP is the low 12 bits (the disassembly's own `.hword 0xXYYY` comment, asm/asm00_2.s:683), and offset 0 is the FIRST ROW of a row-per-level struct -- Mettaur's rows read 0x0028/0x0050/0x0078/0x00A0. Never read 0x003C as 'the Gunner's HP constant': it is element 0, hp 60, row 0 of Gunner's rows.

### statuses (M3) (DERIVED-FROM-CODE (T18, corrected by verifier re-run): per-bit canonical sites measured by walking every object_setFlag/clearFlag/getFlag call and inline flags-field orr/str/tst in reference/bn6f/asm -- written per-bit-store 39/69 (flags1 26/32, flags2 13/32, DAMAGE 0/5; loose any-mask mention 40/69), per-bit readers 37/69 (flags1 24/32, flags2 13/32; loose any-mask mention 43/69), 32 with no per-bit reader, 30 both written and read per-bit; nearest per-status table off_80209EC (data/dat01.s:155, via sub_801A554 asm/asm00_2.s:22211) holds 37 records -- RECORDS: 25 table-only (f4=7+f2=6+f5=6+f6=6) + 12 shared-family (f1=6+f3=6) = 37; MASKS: 4 table-only (0x10000,0x20000,0x40,0x80) + 2 overlap (0x20,0x8) = 6; and 11 distinct directly-written flag2 masks OUTSIDE the table (0x1,0x10,0x100,0x100000,0x2,0x200,0x4,0x4000,0x40000,0x8000,0x80000) -- a mask count, not a record count; table-mask direct sites: 0x8 direct reader asm/asm00_2.s:17482-17485, 0x20 direct setter asm/asm00_2.s:11368 / no reader, 0x40/0x80 neither, 0x10000/0x20000 unmeasured; M3 candidates (per-bit readers): CONFUSED (asm/asm00_2.s:1894-1897) / BLIND (asm/asm00_2.s:16861-16863) / IMMOBILIZED (asm/asm31.s:171386-171390))

Note: T18 verdict, CORRECTED by verifier-hyper re-run (all numbers are generator output; reader/writer rules now strict vs loose, BOTH printed): every bl object_setFlag1/2 + object_clearFlag(2) + inline orr/str to oCollisionData_ObjectFlags1/2 is a setter/clearer; every bl object_getFlag(2) + direct flags-field load followed by a tst/and/lsr mask test is a reader. PER-BIT reader = a mask EQUAL to the bit's own value; ANY-MASK mention = a combined mask that merely carries the bit (a combined test only proves SOME member mattered -- e.g. the 0xa000 BLIND|CONFUSED tst at asm31.s:171391-171393 credits both, the 0x220000 tst at asm00_2.s:23375 credits SUPERARMOR). The M3-facing reader count is the PER-BIT one: 37/69 strict vs 43 loose (loose-only bits: FLINCHING, SUPERARMOR, UNK_29, flags2 bits 11/13/20). Written is likewise per-bit-store: 39/69 strict vs 40 loose -- FLINCHING's only setter credit is the combined 0x400400 orr at asm00_2.s:18308 (playerFlinchAction_80174FE), so per-bit-store it is unwritten. The inline orr/str heuristic is UNSOUND and flagged in code: it fires on ANY ldr/str/ldrh/strh touching the flags field, including a whole-word bulk zero, and credits the first orr/bic within +/-5 lines without checking that the orr targets the flags register (4 credits this run). DAMAGE_* (BattleObject.inc:119-123) have ZERO named sites in asm/ -- they are carried in the damage word by the damage pipeline, so all five stay header-derived. Table reconciliation, three SEPARATE partitions (never added across): RECORDS -- 25 table-only (f4=7 + f2=6 + f5=6 + f6=6) + 12 shared-family (f1=6 + f3=6) = 37; MASKS -- 4 table-only (0x10000,0x20000,0x40,0x80) + 2 overlap (0x20,0x8) = 6; and 11 = the count of DISTINCT directly-written flag2 masks OUTSIDE the table (0x1,0x10,0x100,0x100000,0x2,0x200,0x4,0x4000,0x40000,0x8000,0x80000) -- a mask count, not a record count. Table-mask direct sites measured: 0x8 HAS a direct reader (asm00_2.s:17482-17485: object_getFlag2; mov r1,#8; tst; bne locret), 0x20 has a direct setter (asm00_2.s:11368) and no reader, 0x40/0x80 have neither, 0x10000/0x20000 UNMEASURED for direct sites (budget); the observable read for those is on the flags1 status bit the object.s per-frame status tick propagates (e.g. object.s:5438-5452 clears flags1 0x8000 + flag2 0x80 when the Confused timer Unk_1e expires), so battle_visible is judged on the flags1 bit and is re-derived from the per-bit rule. Duration caveat: the 0x12c=300 at asm00_2.s:11364-11368 is a SHARED 5-second status constant -- off_80141BC also feeds oCollisionData_Unk_1e at 11375-11377 and setInvulnerableTime at 11348/11356 -- do NOT fit 300 to BLIND. M3 scenario candidates (per-bit reader + table-reachable): CONFUSED reader asm/asm00_2.s:1894-1897 (StatusEffectFinal 0x2X -> sub_801A554 family 2 -> flag2 0x80 + timer oCollisionData_Unk_1e -> flags1 0x8000 (tick object.s:5438-5452)); BLIND reader asm/asm00_2.s:16861-16863 (StatusEffectFinal 0x3X -> family 3 -> flag2 0x20 + BlindTimer (inflicter measured: asm/asm00_2.s:11366-11368; NOTE the 0x12c=300 is a SHARED 5-second status duration -- off_80141BC also feeds oCollisionData_Unk_1e at 11375-11377 and setInvulnerableTime at 11348/11356 -- not a BLIND-specific constant)); IMMOBILIZED reader asm/asm31.s:171386-171390 (StatusEffectFinal 0x4X -> family 4 (7 recs) -> flag2 0x40 + timer oCollisionData_Unk_22 -> flags1 0x4000 (tick object.s:5500-5508)). Chip-id caveat: the fixture hand (FIXTURE.md +13) can carry any chip id, but the cannon/wave rows' fired chips inflict no status; which chip id writes which StatusEffectFinal value is NOT pinned here (the setCollisionStatusEffect callers are the asm31.s per-attack effect routines) -- that pinning is M4 work.

### formations (M8) (ROM-WALK (T65): reference/bn6f/bn6f.gba record streams -- off_8020170 encounter tree (asm/asm29.s:10371) + scripted battleSettingsList0 0x080aee70 (bn6f.map:28302) / BattleSettingsList1 0x080b0d88 (bn6f.map:28573, getBattleSettingsFromList0/List1 asm/asm00_1.s:16046-16062), 1240 records over 84 lists (2 scripted + 82 encounter-tree), 1076 0xF0-terminated formation arrays (section denominator = 1076 arrays, all rows status unrecorded) -- old .s parse held 461 records / 297 arrays and missed the whole encounter tree (779 records, 779 arrays); mismatches 0)

Note: T65 ROM-walk audit (supersedes the provisional .s arithmetic): every record stream read from reference/bn6f/bn6f.gba itself -- family A scripted battles (battleSettingsList0 0x080aee70 / BattleSettingsList1 0x080b0d88, bn6f.map:28302/28573, consumed by getBattleSettingsFromList0/List1 asm/asm00_1.s:16046-16062 at index*0x10) = 269+192 = 461 records + 297 0xF0-terminated formation arrays (the old .s parse was exactly right HERE), plus family B random-encounter tree (off_8020170 group tables, selectEncounterTableForMap_80AA5F4 asm/asm29.s:10338-10457: 21 real-world groups / 23 internet groups (23 words proven by 0x080201E4 + 23*4 = 0x08020240 = pt_8020240, asm/asm01.s:555-580), group slot -> 16-word map array -> record list; EVENT_67F/680/681 swap only the internet table) = 82 distinct lists, 779 records, 779 formation arrays -- ALL of it missed by the .s parse (unlabeled ROM after 0x080b1bbc). Totals 84 lists / 1240 records / 1076 formation arrays; every list ends on a 0xff record[0] and every EnemySetupArrPtr reaches a 0xF0 (mismatches 0). Records are 16 bytes (include/rom_structs/BattleSettings.inc; the Background byte +0x4 is proven by battleSettings_setBackground asm/asm03_0.s:14591-14593 strb r0,[BattleSettings_200AF60+0x4]): byte[0]==0xff terminator, byte[4] Background, byte[7] gate handler index (JumpTable80AA6B8 asm29.s:10471), u32@0xc EnemySetupArrPtr -> 4-byte quads, quad[0]==0xF0 stop, quad[2] = enemy id. Formation arrays: 4-byte entries up to the 0xF0 stop consumed by SpawnBattleObjectUsingBattleEntityConfig_8007368.
